That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too fat or too thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really spend much time staring into mirrors at my age. \n\nBut, I suppose... if I do catch a glimpse and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded, I... I just feel sloppy. It's disappointing. I prefer it when... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I've done right by myself. Just... well, I don't want it coming back. \n\nWhen she tries to put butter on things, or sets down a full plate, it... it makes me a bit nervous. I just don't want to start putting weight back on. I've worked to keep things under control, and I'd prefer to keep it that way. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid is... well, that's a strong word. \n\nLike I just said, I just get a bit nervous when she tries to serve me a full plate or... or cook with butter. I just don't want the weight to come back. I've worked to keep things under control, and I just don't want to lose that. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How much do you weigh currently?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned something about it being... too low, I suppose. I don't check the scale all that often myself. \n\nI just know that being a bit lighter... it makes it easier on the joints. Easier to get around the garden, or... or keep up with my walks. That's what matters, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see why everyone is making such a fuss about it, to be honest. \n\nOlder folks like me, we're... well, we're supposed to eat less. It's just natural. I'm still managing all my chores around the house just fine, getting out and about... so I really don't see what the problem is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought we just went over this. I wouldn't use the word anxious. \n\nIt's just... like I said earlier, if my wife tries to cook with butter or hands me a full plate of food, I get a little nervous. I just don't want to start putting the weight back on and lose the control I've worked for. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too fat or too thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really spend much time staring into mirrors at my age. \n\nBut, I suppose... if I do catch a glimpse and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded, I... I just feel sloppy. It's disappointing. I prefer it when... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I've done right by myself. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about that. \"Losing control\" sounds a bit... dramatic, I suppose. \n\nI just... I prefer to keep things the way they are right now. I don't really overthink it much beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating more would lead to you feeling like you're losing control over your eating?\n\nPatient: I... didn't you just ask me that? \n\nNo. I wouldn't say I'd lose control and just start eating everything in sight, if that's what you mean. I just... I don't see the need to eat more than I already do. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel better about yourself if you weighed a bit more\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I... I can't imagine why I would. I feel fine at the weight I am now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned that the number on the scale has gotten a bit too low lately. I don't know about \"significant,\" though. \n\nTo me, being a little lighter just... it makes it easier to get around out in the garden. It's easier on the joints to keep up on my walks. I don't really see the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you frightened by the idea of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose \"frightened\" is a strong word. But... yes, it makes me uncomfortable. When my wife tries to, you know, put butter on my vegetables or hands me a full plate... I get a bit nervous. I just don't want to put the weight back on and... lose the handle I have on things right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that eating a normal or larger amount of food would make you feel like you're unable to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really have the urge to just keep eating, if that's what you mean. I just... I prefer my smaller portions. I've never worried about not being able to stop, I just don't care to eat a large amount in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are a way to maintain control over your life?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know about all that. I wouldn't say it's about control. \n\nI just... I eat what I'm comfortable with. I don't really think about it much deeper than that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I just don't want the weight to come back. I've worked to keep things under control, and I just don't want to lose that. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How much do you weigh currently?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned something about it being... too low, I suppose. I don't check the scale all that often myself. \n\nI just know that being a bit lighter... it makes it easier on the joints. Easier to get around the garden, or... or keep up with my walks. That's what matters, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see why everyone is making such a fuss about it, to be honest. \n\nOlder folks like me, we're... well, we're supposed to eat less. It's just natural. I'm still managing all my chores around the house just fine, getting out and about... so I really don't see what the problem is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought we just went over this. I wouldn't use the word anxious. \n\nIt's just... like I said earlier, if my wife tries to cook with butter or hands me a full plate of food, I get a little nervous. I just don't want to start putting the weight back on and lose the control I've worked for. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too fat or too thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really spend much time staring into mirrors at my age. \n\nBut, I suppose... if I do catch a glimpse and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded, I... I just feel sloppy. It's disappointing. I prefer it when... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I've done right by myself. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about that. \"Losing control\" sounds a bit... dramatic, I suppose. \n\nI just... I prefer to keep things the way they are right now. I don't really overthink it much beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating more would lead to you feeling like you're losing control over your eating?\n\nPatient: I... didn't you just ask me that? \n\nNo. I wouldn't say I'd lose control and just start eating everything in sight, if that's what you mean. I just... I don't see the need to eat more than I already do. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel better about yourself if you weighed a bit more\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I... I can't imagine why I would. I feel fine at the weight I am now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned that the number on the scale has gotten a bit too low lately. I don't know about \"significant,\" though. \n\nTo me, being a little lighter just... it makes it easier to get around out in the garden. It's easier on the joints to keep up on my walks. I don't really see the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you frightened by the idea of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose \"frightened\" is a strong word. But... yes, it makes me uncomfortable. When my wife tries to, you know, put butter on my vegetables or hands me a full plate... I get a bit nervous. I just don't want to put the weight back on and... lose the handle I have on things right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that eating a normal or larger amount of food would make you feel like you're unable to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really have the urge to just keep eating, if that's what you mean. I just... I prefer my smaller portions. I've never worried about not being able to stop, I just don't care to eat a large amount in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are a way to maintain control over your life?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know about all that. I wouldn't say it's about control. \n\nI just... I eat what I'm comfortable with. I don't really think about it much deeper than that.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to limit your food intake mostly because you are worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. Like I mentioned a minute ago... I just get anxious about putting the weight back on. When my wife gives me those full plates, or tries to add things... I just don't want to lose what I've worked to keep. That's really all it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... it was my wife's idea, really. She thinks I'm not eating enough. \n\nI don't see what the fuss is about. Most days I just have my coffee and a piece of toast in the morning... maybe some soup or a few vegetables later on. Big meals just... they feel unnecessary at my age. But she insisted I come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Not exactly trying to lose more, I suppose. Just... well, I don't want it coming back. \n\nWhen she tries to put butter on things, or sets down a full plate, it... it makes me a bit nervous. I just don't want to start putting weight back on. I've worked to keep things under control, and I'd prefer to keep it that way. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid is... well, that's a strong word. \n\nLike I just said, I just get a bit nervous when she tries to serve me a full plate or... or cook with butter. I just don't want the weight to come back. I've worked to keep things under control, and I just don't want to lose that. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How much do you weigh currently?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned something about it being... too low, I suppose. I don't check the scale all that often myself. \n\nI just know that being a bit lighter... it makes it easier on the joints. Easier to get around the garden, or... or keep up with my walks. That's what matters, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see why everyone is making such a fuss about it, to be honest. \n\nOlder folks like me, we're... well, we're supposed to eat less. It's just natural. I'm still managing all my chores around the house just fine, getting out and about... so I really don't see what the problem is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought we just went over this. I wouldn't use the word anxious. \n\nIt's just... like I said earlier, if my wife tries to cook with butter or hands me a full plate of food, I get a little nervous. I just don't want to start putting the weight back on and lose the control I've worked for. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too fat or too thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really spend much time staring into mirrors at my age. \n\nBut, I suppose... if I do catch a glimpse and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded, I... I just feel sloppy. It's disappointing. I prefer it when... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I've done right by myself. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about that. \"Losing control\" sounds a bit... dramatic, I suppose. \n\nI just... I prefer to keep things the way they are right now. I don't really overthink it much beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating more would lead to you feeling like you're losing control over your eating?\n\nPatient: I... didn't you just ask me that? \n\nNo. I wouldn't say I'd lose control and just start eating everything in sight, if that's what you mean. I just... I don't see the need to eat more than I already do. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel better about yourself if you weighed a bit more\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I... I can't imagine why I would. I feel fine at the weight I am now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned that the number on the scale has gotten a bit too low lately. I don't know about \"significant,\" though. \n\nTo me, being a little lighter just... it makes it easier to get around out in the garden. It's easier on the joints to keep up on my walks. I don't really see the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you frightened by the idea of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose \"frightened\" is a strong word. But... yes, it makes me uncomfortable. When my wife tries to, you know, put butter on my vegetables or hands me a full plate... I get a bit nervous. I just don't want to put the weight back on and... lose the handle I have on things right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that eating a normal or larger amount of food would make you feel like you're unable to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really have the urge to just keep eating, if that's what you mean. I just... I prefer my smaller portions. I've never worried about not being able to stop, I just don't care to eat a large amount in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are a way to maintain control over your life?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know about all that. I wouldn't say it's about control. \n\nI just... I eat what I'm comfortable with. I don't really think about it much deeper than that.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to limit your food intake mostly because you are worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. Like I mentioned a minute ago... I just get anxious about putting the weight back on. When my wife gives me those full plates, or tries to add things... I just don't want to lose what I've worked to keep. That's really all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be happier if you were thinner than you are now?\n\nPatient: Happier? I... I don't know if I'd use that word. \n\nIt's just... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I guess I feel like I've done right by myself. Kept things in line. But... if I look in the mirror and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded... I don't like it. It just makes me feel sloppy. Disappointed, I suppose. \n\nSo... I just prefer to keep it off.\n\nDoctor: Is your main goal in limiting your food intake to avoid gaining weight or to achieve a specific thinness?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said... I don't have a specific number or anything in mind. It's mostly just about not gaining it back. \n\nWhen my wife tries to put butter on things, or hands me one of those full plates... I just want things to stay how they are.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... it was my wife's idea, really. She thinks I'm not eating enough. \n\nI don't see what the fuss is about. Most days I just have my coffee and a piece of toast in the morning... maybe some soup or a few vegetables later on. Big meals just... they feel unnecessary at my age. But she insisted I come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Not exactly trying to lose more, I suppose. Just... well, I don't want it coming back. \n\nWhen she tries to put butter on things, or sets down a full plate, it... it makes me a bit nervous. I just don't want to start putting weight back on. I've worked to keep things under control, and I'd prefer to keep it that way. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid is... well, that's a strong word. \n\nLike I just said, I just get a bit nervous when she tries to serve me a full plate or... or cook with butter. I just don't want the weight to come back. I've worked to keep things under control, and I just don't want to lose that. That's all.\n\nDoctor: How much do you weigh currently?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned something about it being... too low, I suppose. I don't check the scale all that often myself. \n\nI just know that being a bit lighter... it makes it easier on the joints. Easier to get around the garden, or... or keep up with my walks. That's what matters, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really see why everyone is making such a fuss about it, to be honest. \n\nOlder folks like me, we're... well, we're supposed to eat less. It's just natural. I'm still managing all my chores around the house just fine, getting out and about... so I really don't see what the problem is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought we just went over this. I wouldn't use the word anxious. \n\nIt's just... like I said earlier, if my wife tries to cook with butter or hands me a full plate of food, I get a little nervous. I just don't want to start putting the weight back on and lose the control I've worked for. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too fat or too thin when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really spend much time staring into mirrors at my age. \n\nBut, I suppose... if I do catch a glimpse and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded, I... I just feel sloppy. It's disappointing. I prefer it when... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I've done right by myself. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know about that. \"Losing control\" sounds a bit... dramatic, I suppose. \n\nI just... I prefer to keep things the way they are right now. I don't really overthink it much beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating more would lead to you feeling like you're losing control over your eating?\n\nPatient: I... didn't you just ask me that? \n\nNo. I wouldn't say I'd lose control and just start eating everything in sight, if that's what you mean. I just... I don't see the need to eat more than I already do. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel better about yourself if you weighed a bit more\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I... I can't imagine why I would. I feel fine at the weight I am now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well... my regular doctor mentioned that the number on the scale has gotten a bit too low lately. I don't know about \"significant,\" though. \n\nTo me, being a little lighter just... it makes it easier to get around out in the garden. It's easier on the joints to keep up on my walks. I don't really see the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you frightened by the idea of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose \"frightened\" is a strong word. But... yes, it makes me uncomfortable. When my wife tries to, you know, put butter on my vegetables or hands me a full plate... I get a bit nervous. I just don't want to put the weight back on and... lose the handle I have on things right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that eating a normal or larger amount of food would make you feel like you're unable to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really have the urge to just keep eating, if that's what you mean. I just... I prefer my smaller portions. I've never worried about not being able to stop, I just don't care to eat a large amount in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are a way to maintain control over your life?\n\nPatient: I... don't really know about all that. I wouldn't say it's about control. \n\nI just... I eat what I'm comfortable with. I don't really think about it much deeper than that.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to limit your food intake mostly because you are worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. Like I mentioned a minute ago... I just get anxious about putting the weight back on. When my wife gives me those full plates, or tries to add things... I just don't want to lose what I've worked to keep. That's really all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be happier if you were thinner than you are now?\n\nPatient: Happier? I... I don't know if I'd use that word. \n\nIt's just... well, if my belt feels a notch looser, I guess I feel like I've done right by myself. Kept things in line. But... if I look in the mirror and my stomach looks even the least bit rounded... I don't like it. It just makes me feel sloppy. Disappointed, I suppose. \n\nSo... I just prefer to keep it off.\n\nDoctor: Is your main goal in limiting your food intake to avoid gaining weight or to achieve a specific thinness?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said... I don't have a specific number or anything in mind. It's mostly just about not gaining it back. \n\nWhen my wife tries to put butter on things, or hands me one of those full plates... I just get nervous. I worry I'll start putting the weight back on and lose the control I've worked to keep. I just want things to stay how they are.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat so little mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought I just answered that, Doc. Yes. \n\nLike I was just saying... when she adds butter to my food, or hands me one of those plates piled high... it just makes me nervous. I don't want to put the weight back on and lose the grip I've got on things. That's really all there is to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look fat even when others tell you that you are too thin?\n\nPatient: Fat? No, I... I don't think so. \n\nMy wife fusses, sure, but... I don't look in the mirror and see a fat man, if that's what you're asking. I just... I just look like me, I guess. I don't really spend much time staring at myself anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that if you started eating more, you would be unable to stop eating?\n\nPatient: Unable to stop? ... No, I don't think so. \n\nI wouldn't do that. I'm not... I'm not worried about gorging myself, if that's what you mean. I just prefer to eat what I'm used to, that's all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
